    def lire_go(chemin: Path) -> dict[str, dict]:
        """Table Markdown : | id | accordé le | type | périmètre | plafond | échéance | accordé par | référence |"""
        if not chemin.is_file():
            bloquer(f"registre {chemin} absent : aucun GO ne peut être établi")
        go = {}
        for n, ligne in enumerate(chemin.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines(), 1):
            if not LIGNE_GO.match(ligne):
                continue
            cellules = [c.strip() for c in ligne.strip().strip("|").split("|")]
            if len(cellules) != 8:
                bloquer(f"registre GO mal formé ligne {n} : 8 colonnes attendues, {len(cellules)} trouvées")
            ident, accorde, typ, perimetre, plafond, echeance, par, ref = cellules
            try:
                accorde_d = date.fromisoformat(accorde)
                # « — » : sans échéance, admis seulement pour un GO qui n'est pas de calcul (GO-2026-10-07-01, dépôt
                # distant) ; un GO de calcul a toujours une échéance lisible (relecture du lancement de la revalidation, L-2)
>               echeance_d = None if (echeance == "—" and typ != "calcul") else date.fromisoformat(echeance)
                                                                                ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^

    def verifier_go(chemin_registre: Path, chemin_garde: Path, go_id: str, plafond_usd: float,
                    aujourd_hui: dt.date) -> dict:
        """GO de calcul valide selon la garde de `main` (type, Lazar, validité, révocation, extensions), montant exact.
    
        La garde est appelée telle quelle (`decider`) sur une commande de lancement qui cite le GO : son refus (sortie 2, motif
        sur la sortie d'erreur) devient un `Refus` avec le même motif. Montant : la cellule « plafond » de la première ligne et de
        chaque extension doit valoir exactement `plafond_usd` USD (une extension qui change le plafond demande un texte neuf).
        """
        if go_id != GO_ATTENDU:
            raise Refus(f"GO {go_id} : le texte v3 nomme {GO_ATTENDU} (aucun autre GO)")
        garde = charger_garde(chemin_garde, chemin_registre)
        tampon = io.StringIO()
        try:
            with redirect_stderr(tampon):
                garde.decider(f"GO_ID={go_id} vastai create instance 0", aujourd_hui)
        except SystemExit as e:
            if e.code not in (0, None):
>               raise Refus(f"garde de main : {tampon.getvalue().strip()}") from None

chemin_registre = PosixPath('/home/user/controle-ia-travail/registres/go.md')

    def verifier_go_machine(chemin_registre: Path, chemin_garde: Path, go_id: str, aujourd_hui: dt.date) -> dict:
        """Ligne de GO de la machine, nommée par `GO_ID_MACHINE` : forme, garde de `main` (type calcul, Lazar, validité, révocation,
        extensions), ≠ GO des appels, **plafond lu sur la ligne** (une seule valeur sur la ligne et ses extensions), **périmètre lu**
        (`MOTS_PERIMETRE_MACHINE`). Rend le plafond en USD avec la ligne."""
        if not isinstance(go_id, str) or not MOTIF_GO.match(go_id):
            raise h.Refus(f"{NOM_GO_MACHINE_ENV} absent ou illisible ({go_id!r}) : GO-AAAA-MM-JJ-NN exigé")
        if go_id == GO_COURSE:
            raise h.Refus(f"{NOM_GO_MACHINE_ENV}={go_id} est la ligne des appels : la machine a sa propre ligne de GO")
        garde = h.charger_garde(chemin_garde, chemin_registre)
        tampon = io.StringIO()
        try:
            with redirect_stderr(tampon):
                garde.decider(f"GO_ID={go_id} vastai create instance 0", aujourd_hui)
        except SystemExit as e:
            if e.code not in (0, None):
>               raise h.Refus(f"garde de main ({go_id}) : {tampon.getvalue().strip()}") from None

kwargs = {'env': {'TMPDIR': '/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-controle-ia-travail/7e8b3f41-3765-57f1-9b67-e1297e8214ec/scratchpad/routine/tmp-tests', 'PYTHONPATH': 'src', 'IS_SANDBOX': '1', 'SHELL': '/bin/bash', ...}, 'stdout': -1, 'stderr': -1}

    def run(*popenargs,
            input=None, capture_output=False, timeout=None, check=False, **kwargs):
        """Run command with arguments and return a CompletedProcess instance.
    
        The returned instance will have attributes args, returncode, stdout and
        stderr. By default, stdout and stderr are not captured, and those attributes
        will be None. Pass stdout=PIPE and/or stderr=PIPE in order to capture them,
        or pass capture_output=True to capture both.
    
        If check is True and the exit code was non-zero, it raises a
        CalledProcessError. The CalledProcessError object will have the return code
        in the returncode attribute, and output & stderr attributes if those streams
        were captured.
    
        If timeout (seconds) is given and the process takes too long,
         a TimeoutExpired exception will be raised.
    
        There is an optional argument "input", allowing you to
        pass bytes or a string to the subprocess's stdin.  If you use this argument
        you may not also use the Popen constructor's "stdin" argument, as
        it will be used internally.
    
        By default, all communication is in bytes, and therefore any "input" should
        be bytes, and the stdout and stderr will be bytes. If in text mode, any
        "input" should be a string, and stdout and stderr will be strings decoded
        according to locale encoding, or by "encoding" if set. Text mode is
        triggered by setting any of text, encoding, errors or universal_newlines.
    
        The other arguments are the same as for the Popen constructor.
        """
        if input is not None:
            if kwargs.get('stdin') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdin and input arguments may not both be used.')
            kwargs['stdin'] = PIPE
    
        if capture_output:
            if kwargs.get('stdout') is not None or kwargs.get('stderr') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdout and stderr arguments may not be used '
                                 'with capture_output.')
            kwargs['stdout'] = PIPE
            kwargs['stderr'] = PIPE
    
        with Popen(*popenargs, **kwargs) as process:
            try:
                stdout, stderr = process.communicate(input, timeout=timeout)
            except TimeoutExpired as exc:
                process.kill()
                if _mswindows:
                    # Windows accumulates the output in a single blocking
                    # read() call run on child threads, with the timeout
                    # being done in a join() on those threads.  communicate()
                    # _after_ kill() is required to collect that and add it
                    # to the exception.
                    exc.stdout, exc.stderr = process.communicate()
                else:
                    # POSIX _communicate already populated the output so
                    # far into the TimeoutExpired exception.
                    process.wait()
                raise
            except:  # Including KeyboardInterrupt, communicate handled that.
                process.kill()
                # We don't call process.wait() as .__exit__ does that for us.
                raise
            retcode = process.poll()
            if check and retcode:
>               raise CalledProcessError(retcode, process.args,
                                         output=stdout, stderr=stderr)

                    # read() call run on child threads, with the timeout
                    # being done in a join() on those threads.  communicate()
                    # _after_ kill() is required to collect that and add it
                    # to the exception.
                    exc.stdout, exc.stderr = process.communicate()
                else:
                    # POSIX _communicate already populated the output so
                    # far into the TimeoutExpired exception.
                    process.wait()
                raise
            except:  # Including KeyboardInterrupt, communicate handled that.
                process.kill()
                # We don't call process.wait() as .__exit__ does that for us.
                raise
            retcode = process.poll()
            if check and retcode:
>               raise CalledProcessError(retcode, process.args,
                                         output=stdout, stderr=stderr)
E               subprocess.CalledProcessError: Command '['git', '-C', '/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-controle-ia-travail/7e8b3f41-3765-57f1-9b67-e1297e8214ec/scratchpad/routine/tmp-tests/pytest-of-root/pytest-0/course-R-01/depot', '-c', 'user.name=essai', '-c', 'user.email=essai@exemple.invalid', '-c', 'commit.gpgsign=false', 'read-tree', '5e5cb65b4daf8726ebe1c4032a836b850de7ea2f']' returned non-zero exit status 128.

input = None, capture_output = True, timeout = None, check = True
popenargs = (['git', '-C', '/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-controle-ia-travail/7e8b3f41-3765-57f1-9b67-e1297e8214ec/scratchpad/routine/tmp-tests/pytest-of-root/pytest-0/course-S-01/depot', '-c', 'user.name=essai', '-c', ...],)
kwargs = {'env': {'TMPDIR': '/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-controle-ia-travail/7e8b3f41-3765-57f1-9b67-e1297e8214ec/scratchpad/routine/tmp-tests', 'PYTHONPATH': 'src', 'IS_SANDBOX': '1', 'SHELL': '/bin/bash', ...}, 'stdout': -1, 'stderr': -1}

    def run(*popenargs,
            input=None, capture_output=False, timeout=None, check=False, **kwargs):
        """Run command with arguments and return a CompletedProcess instance.
    
        The returned instance will have attributes args, returncode, stdout and
        stderr. By default, stdout and stderr are not captured, and those attributes
        will be None. Pass stdout=PIPE and/or stderr=PIPE in order to capture them,
        or pass capture_output=True to capture both.
    
        If check is True and the exit code was non-zero, it raises a
        CalledProcessError. The CalledProcessError object will have the return code
        in the returncode attribute, and output & stderr attributes if those streams
        were captured.
    
        If timeout (seconds) is given and the process takes too long,
         a TimeoutExpired exception will be raised.
    
        There is an optional argument "input", allowing you to
        pass bytes or a string to the subprocess's stdin.  If you use this argument
        you may not also use the Popen constructor's "stdin" argument, as
        it will be used internally.
    
        By default, all communication is in bytes, and therefore any "input" should
        be bytes, and the stdout and stderr will be bytes. If in text mode, any
        "input" should be a string, and stdout and stderr will be strings decoded
        according to locale encoding, or by "encoding" if set. Text mode is
        triggered by setting any of text, encoding, errors or universal_newlines.
    
        The other arguments are the same as for the Popen constructor.
        """
        if input is not None:
            if kwargs.get('stdin') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdin and input arguments may not both be used.')
            kwargs['stdin'] = PIPE
    
        if capture_output:
            if kwargs.get('stdout') is not None or kwargs.get('stderr') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdout and stderr arguments may not be used '
                                 'with capture_output.')
            kwargs['stdout'] = PIPE
            kwargs['stderr'] = PIPE
    
        with Popen(*popenargs, **kwargs) as process:
            try:
                stdout, stderr = process.communicate(input, timeout=timeout)
            except TimeoutExpired as exc:
                process.kill()
                if _mswindows:
                    # Windows accumulates the output in a single blocking
                    # read() call run on child threads, with the timeout
                    # being done in a join() on those threads.  communicate()
                    # _after_ kill() is required to collect that and add it
                    # to the exception.
                    exc.stdout, exc.stderr = process.communicate()
                else:
                    # POSIX _communicate already populated the output so
                    # far into the TimeoutExpired exception.
                    process.wait()
                raise
            except:  # Including KeyboardInterrupt, communicate handled that.
                process.kill()
                # We don't call process.wait() as .__exit__ does that for us.
                raise
            retcode = process.poll()
            if check and retcode:
>               raise CalledProcessError(retcode, process.args,
                                         output=stdout, stderr=stderr)
E               subprocess.CalledProcessError: Command '['git', '-C', '/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-controle-ia-travail/7e8b3f41-3765-57f1-9b67-e1297e8214ec/scratchpad/routine/tmp-tests/pytest-of-root/pytest-0/course-S-01/depot', '-c', 'user.name=essai', '-c', 'user.email=essai@exemple.invalid', '-c', 'commit.gpgsign=false', 'read-tree', '5e5cb65b4daf8726ebe1c4032a836b850de7ea2f']' returned non-zero exit status 128.

    def run(*popenargs,
            input=None, capture_output=False, timeout=None, check=False, **kwargs):
        """Run command with arguments and return a CompletedProcess instance.
    
        The returned instance will have attributes args, returncode, stdout and
        stderr. By default, stdout and stderr are not captured, and those attributes
        will be None. Pass stdout=PIPE and/or stderr=PIPE in order to capture them,
        or pass capture_output=True to capture both.
    
        If check is True and the exit code was non-zero, it raises a
        CalledProcessError. The CalledProcessError object will have the return code
        in the returncode attribute, and output & stderr attributes if those streams
        were captured.
    
        If timeout (seconds) is given and the process takes too long,
         a TimeoutExpired exception will be raised.
    
        There is an optional argument "input", allowing you to
        pass bytes or a string to the subprocess's stdin.  If you use this argument
        you may not also use the Popen constructor's "stdin" argument, as
        it will be used internally.
    
        By default, all communication is in bytes, and therefore any "input" should
        be bytes, and the stdout and stderr will be bytes. If in text mode, any
        "input" should be a string, and stdout and stderr will be strings decoded
        according to locale encoding, or by "encoding" if set. Text mode is
        triggered by setting any of text, encoding, errors or universal_newlines.
    
        The other arguments are the same as for the Popen constructor.
        """
        if input is not None:
            if kwargs.get('stdin') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdin and input arguments may not both be used.')
            kwargs['stdin'] = PIPE
    
        if capture_output:
            if kwargs.get('stdout') is not None or kwargs.get('stderr') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdout and stderr arguments may not be used '
                                 'with capture_output.')
            kwargs['stdout'] = PIPE
            kwargs['stderr'] = PIPE
    
        with Popen(*popenargs, **kwargs) as process:
            try:
                stdout, stderr = process.communicate(input, timeout=timeout)
            except TimeoutExpired as exc:
                process.kill()
                if _mswindows:
                    # Windows accumulates the output in a single blocking
                    # read() call run on child threads, with the timeout
                    # being done in a join() on those threads.  communicate()
                    # _after_ kill() is required to collect that and add it
                    # to the exception.
                    exc.stdout, exc.stderr = process.communicate()
                else:
                    # POSIX _communicate already populated the output so
                    # far into the TimeoutExpired exception.
                    process.wait()
                raise
            except:  # Including KeyboardInterrupt, communicate handled that.
                process.kill()
                # We don't call process.wait() as .__exit__ does that for us.
                raise
            retcode = process.poll()
            if check and retcode:
>               raise CalledProcessError(retcode, process.args,
                                         output=stdout, stderr=stderr)
E               subprocess.CalledProcessError: Command '['git', '-C', '/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-controle-ia-travail/7e8b3f41-3765-57f1-9b67-e1297e8214ec/scratchpad/routine/tmp-tests/pytest-of-root/pytest-0/course-S-02/depot', '-c', 'user.name=essai', '-c', 'user.email=essai@exemple.invalid', '-c', 'commit.gpgsign=false', 'read-tree', '5e5cb65b4daf8726ebe1c4032a836b850de7ea2f']' returned non-zero exit status 128.

kwargs = {'env': {'TMPDIR': '/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-controle-ia-travail/7e8b3f41-3765-57f1-9b67-e1297e8214ec/scratchpad/routine/tmp-tests', 'PYTHONPATH': 'src', 'IS_SANDBOX': '1', 'SHELL': '/bin/bash', ...}, 'stdout': -1, 'stderr': -1}

    def run(*popenargs,
            input=None, capture_output=False, timeout=None, check=False, **kwargs):
        """Run command with arguments and return a CompletedProcess instance.
    
        The returned instance will have attributes args, returncode, stdout and
        stderr. By default, stdout and stderr are not captured, and those attributes
        will be None. Pass stdout=PIPE and/or stderr=PIPE in order to capture them,
        or pass capture_output=True to capture both.
    
        If check is True and the exit code was non-zero, it raises a
        CalledProcessError. The CalledProcessError object will have the return code
        in the returncode attribute, and output & stderr attributes if those streams
        were captured.
    
        If timeout (seconds) is given and the process takes too long,
         a TimeoutExpired exception will be raised.
    
        There is an optional argument "input", allowing you to
        pass bytes or a string to the subprocess's stdin.  If you use this argument
        you may not also use the Popen constructor's "stdin" argument, as
        it will be used internally.
    
        By default, all communication is in bytes, and therefore any "input" should
        be bytes, and the stdout and stderr will be bytes. If in text mode, any
        "input" should be a string, and stdout and stderr will be strings decoded
        according to locale encoding, or by "encoding" if set. Text mode is
        triggered by setting any of text, encoding, errors or universal_newlines.
    
        The other arguments are the same as for the Popen constructor.
        """
        if input is not None:
            if kwargs.get('stdin') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdin and input arguments may not both be used.')
            kwargs['stdin'] = PIPE
    
        if capture_output:
            if kwargs.get('stdout') is not None or kwargs.get('stderr') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdout and stderr arguments may not be used '
                                 'with capture_output.')
            kwargs['stdout'] = PIPE
            kwargs['stderr'] = PIPE
    
        with Popen(*popenargs, **kwargs) as process:
            try:
                stdout, stderr = process.communicate(input, timeout=timeout)
            except TimeoutExpired as exc:
                process.kill()
                if _mswindows:
                    # Windows accumulates the output in a single blocking
                    # read() call run on child threads, with the timeout
                    # being done in a join() on those threads.  communicate()
                    # _after_ kill() is required to collect that and add it
                    # to the exception.
                    exc.stdout, exc.stderr = process.communicate()
                else:
                    # POSIX _communicate already populated the output so
                    # far into the TimeoutExpired exception.
                    process.wait()
                raise
            except:  # Including KeyboardInterrupt, communicate handled that.
                process.kill()
                # We don't call process.wait() as .__exit__ does that for us.
                raise
            retcode = process.poll()
            if check and retcode:
>               raise CalledProcessError(retcode, process.args,
                                         output=stdout, stderr=stderr)
E               subprocess.CalledProcessError: Command '['git', '-C', '/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-controle-ia-travail/7e8b3f41-3765-57f1-9b67-e1297e8214ec/scratchpad/routine/tmp-tests/pytest-of-root/pytest-0/course-S-04/depot', '-c', 'user.name=essai', '-c', 'user.email=essai@exemple.invalid', '-c', 'commit.gpgsign=false', 'read-tree', '5e5cb65b4daf8726ebe1c4032a836b850de7ea2f']' returned non-zero exit status 128.

    def run(*popenargs,
            input=None, capture_output=False, timeout=None, check=False, **kwargs):
        """Run command with arguments and return a CompletedProcess instance.
    
        The returned instance will have attributes args, returncode, stdout and
        stderr. By default, stdout and stderr are not captured, and those attributes
        will be None. Pass stdout=PIPE and/or stderr=PIPE in order to capture them,
        or pass capture_output=True to capture both.
    
        If check is True and the exit code was non-zero, it raises a
        CalledProcessError. The CalledProcessError object will have the return code
        in the returncode attribute, and output & stderr attributes if those streams
        were captured.
    
        If timeout (seconds) is given and the process takes too long,
         a TimeoutExpired exception will be raised.
    
        There is an optional argument "input", allowing you to
        pass bytes or a string to the subprocess's stdin.  If you use this argument
        you may not also use the Popen constructor's "stdin" argument, as
        it will be used internally.
    
        By default, all communication is in bytes, and therefore any "input" should
        be bytes, and the stdout and stderr will be bytes. If in text mode, any
        "input" should be a string, and stdout and stderr will be strings decoded
        according to locale encoding, or by "encoding" if set. Text mode is
        triggered by setting any of text, encoding, errors or universal_newlines.
    
        The other arguments are the same as for the Popen constructor.
        """
        if input is not None:
            if kwargs.get('stdin') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdin and input arguments may not both be used.')
            kwargs['stdin'] = PIPE
    
        if capture_output:
            if kwargs.get('stdout') is not None or kwargs.get('stderr') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdout and stderr arguments may not be used '
                                 'with capture_output.')
            kwargs['stdout'] = PIPE
            kwargs['stderr'] = PIPE
    
        with Popen(*popenargs, **kwargs) as process:
            try:
                stdout, stderr = process.communicate(input, timeout=timeout)
            except TimeoutExpired as exc:
                process.kill()
                if _mswindows:
                    # Windows accumulates the output in a single blocking
                    # read() call run on child threads, with the timeout
                    # being done in a join() on those threads.  communicate()
                    # _after_ kill() is required to collect that and add it
                    # to the exception.
                    exc.stdout, exc.stderr = process.communicate()
                else:
                    # POSIX _communicate already populated the output so
                    # far into the TimeoutExpired exception.
                    process.wait()
                raise
            except:  # Including KeyboardInterrupt, communicate handled that.
                process.kill()
                # We don't call process.wait() as .__exit__ does that for us.
                raise
            retcode = process.poll()
            if check and retcode:
>               raise CalledProcessError(retcode, process.args,
                                         output=stdout, stderr=stderr)
E               subprocess.CalledProcessError: Command '['git', '-C', '/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-controle-ia-travail/7e8b3f41-3765-57f1-9b67-e1297e8214ec/scratchpad/routine/tmp-tests/pytest-of-root/pytest-0/course-S-08/depot', '-c', 'user.name=essai', '-c', 'user.email=essai@exemple.invalid', '-c', 'commit.gpgsign=false', 'read-tree', '5e5cb65b4daf8726ebe1c4032a836b850de7ea2f']' returned non-zero exit status 128.

input = None, capture_output = True, timeout = None, check = True
popenargs = (['git', '-C', '/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-controle-ia-travail/7e8b3f41-3765-57f1-9b67-e1297e8214ec/scratchpad/routine/tmp-tests/pytest-of-root/pytest-0/course-S-013/depot', '-c', 'user.name=essai', '-c', ...],)
kwargs = {'env': {'TMPDIR': '/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-controle-ia-travail/7e8b3f41-3765-57f1-9b67-e1297e8214ec/scratchpad/routine/tmp-tests', 'PYTHONPATH': 'src', 'IS_SANDBOX': '1', 'SHELL': '/bin/bash', ...}, 'stdout': -1, 'stderr': -1}

    def run(*popenargs,
            input=None, capture_output=False, timeout=None, check=False, **kwargs):
        """Run command with arguments and return a CompletedProcess instance.
    
        The returned instance will have attributes args, returncode, stdout and
        stderr. By default, stdout and stderr are not captured, and those attributes
        will be None. Pass stdout=PIPE and/or stderr=PIPE in order to capture them,
        or pass capture_output=True to capture both.
    
        If check is True and the exit code was non-zero, it raises a
        CalledProcessError. The CalledProcessError object will have the return code
        in the returncode attribute, and output & stderr attributes if those streams
        were captured.
    
        If timeout (seconds) is given and the process takes too long,
         a TimeoutExpired exception will be raised.
    
        There is an optional argument "input", allowing you to
        pass bytes or a string to the subprocess's stdin.  If you use this argument
        you may not also use the Popen constructor's "stdin" argument, as
        it will be used internally.
    
        By default, all communication is in bytes, and therefore any "input" should
        be bytes, and the stdout and stderr will be bytes. If in text mode, any
        "input" should be a string, and stdout and stderr will be strings decoded
        according to locale encoding, or by "encoding" if set. Text mode is
        triggered by setting any of text, encoding, errors or universal_newlines.
    
        The other arguments are the same as for the Popen constructor.
        """
        if input is not None:
            if kwargs.get('stdin') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdin and input arguments may not both be used.')
            kwargs['stdin'] = PIPE
    
        if capture_output:
            if kwargs.get('stdout') is not None or kwargs.get('stderr') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdout and stderr arguments may not be used '
                                 'with capture_output.')
            kwargs['stdout'] = PIPE
            kwargs['stderr'] = PIPE
    
        with Popen(*popenargs, **kwargs) as process:
            try:
                stdout, stderr = process.communicate(input, timeout=timeout)
            except TimeoutExpired as exc:
                process.kill()
                if _mswindows:
                    # Windows accumulates the output in a single blocking
                    # read() call run on child threads, with the timeout
                    # being done in a join() on those threads.  communicate()
                    # _after_ kill() is required to collect that and add it
                    # to the exception.
                    exc.stdout, exc.stderr = process.communicate()
                else:
                    # POSIX _communicate already populated the output so
                    # far into the TimeoutExpired exception.
                    process.wait()
                raise
            except:  # Including KeyboardInterrupt, communicate handled that.
                process.kill()
                # We don't call process.wait() as .__exit__ does that for us.
                raise
            retcode = process.poll()
            if check and retcode:
>               raise CalledProcessError(retcode, process.args,
                                         output=stdout, stderr=stderr)
E               subprocess.CalledProcessError: Command '['git', '-C', '/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-controle-ia-travail/7e8b3f41-3765-57f1-9b67-e1297e8214ec/scratchpad/routine/tmp-tests/pytest-of-root/pytest-0/course-S-013/depot', '-c', 'user.name=essai', '-c', 'user.email=essai@exemple.invalid', '-c', 'commit.gpgsign=false', 'read-tree', '5e5cb65b4daf8726ebe1c4032a836b850de7ea2f']' returned non-zero exit status 128.

    def run(*popenargs,
            input=None, capture_output=False, timeout=None, check=False, **kwargs):
        """Run command with arguments and return a CompletedProcess instance.
    
        The returned instance will have attributes args, returncode, stdout and
        stderr. By default, stdout and stderr are not captured, and those attributes
        will be None. Pass stdout=PIPE and/or stderr=PIPE in order to capture them,
        or pass capture_output=True to capture both.
    
        If check is True and the exit code was non-zero, it raises a
        CalledProcessError. The CalledProcessError object will have the return code
        in the returncode attribute, and output & stderr attributes if those streams
        were captured.
    
        If timeout (seconds) is given and the process takes too long,
         a TimeoutExpired exception will be raised.
    
        There is an optional argument "input", allowing you to
        pass bytes or a string to the subprocess's stdin.  If you use this argument
        you may not also use the Popen constructor's "stdin" argument, as
        it will be used internally.
    
        By default, all communication is in bytes, and therefore any "input" should
        be bytes, and the stdout and stderr will be bytes. If in text mode, any
        "input" should be a string, and stdout and stderr will be strings decoded
        according to locale encoding, or by "encoding" if set. Text mode is
        triggered by setting any of text, encoding, errors or universal_newlines.
    
        The other arguments are the same as for the Popen constructor.
        """
        if input is not None:
            if kwargs.get('stdin') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdin and input arguments may not both be used.')
            kwargs['stdin'] = PIPE
    
        if capture_output:
            if kwargs.get('stdout') is not None or kwargs.get('stderr') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdout and stderr arguments may not be used '
                                 'with capture_output.')
            kwargs['stdout'] = PIPE
            kwargs['stderr'] = PIPE
    
        with Popen(*popenargs, **kwargs) as process:
            try:
                stdout, stderr = process.communicate(input, timeout=timeout)
            except TimeoutExpired as exc:
                process.kill()
                if _mswindows:
                    # Windows accumulates the output in a single blocking
                    # read() call run on child threads, with the timeout
                    # being done in a join() on those threads.  communicate()
                    # _after_ kill() is required to collect that and add it
                    # to the exception.
                    exc.stdout, exc.stderr = process.communicate()
                else:
                    # POSIX _communicate already populated the output so
                    # far into the TimeoutExpired exception.
                    process.wait()
                raise
            except:  # Including KeyboardInterrupt, communicate handled that.
                process.kill()
                # We don't call process.wait() as .__exit__ does that for us.
                raise
            retcode = process.poll()
            if check and retcode:
>               raise CalledProcessError(retcode, process.args,
                                         output=stdout, stderr=stderr)
E               subprocess.CalledProcessError: Command '['git', '-C', '/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-controle-ia-travail/7e8b3f41-3765-57f1-9b67-e1297e8214ec/scratchpad/routine/tmp-tests/pytest-of-root/pytest-0/course-S-014/depot', '-c', 'user.name=essai', '-c', 'user.email=essai@exemple.invalid', '-c', 'commit.gpgsign=false', 'read-tree', '5e5cb65b4daf8726ebe1c4032a836b850de7ea2f']' returned non-zero exit status 128.

kwargs = {'env': {'TMPDIR': '/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-controle-ia-travail/7e8b3f41-3765-57f1-9b67-e1297e8214ec/scratchpad/routine/tmp-tests', 'PYTHONPATH': 'src', 'IS_SANDBOX': '1', 'SHELL': '/bin/bash', ...}, 'stdout': -1, 'stderr': -1}

    def run(*popenargs,
            input=None, capture_output=False, timeout=None, check=False, **kwargs):
        """Run command with arguments and return a CompletedProcess instance.
    
        The returned instance will have attributes args, returncode, stdout and
        stderr. By default, stdout and stderr are not captured, and those attributes
        will be None. Pass stdout=PIPE and/or stderr=PIPE in order to capture them,
        or pass capture_output=True to capture both.
    
        If check is True and the exit code was non-zero, it raises a
        CalledProcessError. The CalledProcessError object will have the return code
        in the returncode attribute, and output & stderr attributes if those streams
        were captured.
    
        If timeout (seconds) is given and the process takes too long,
         a TimeoutExpired exception will be raised.
    
        There is an optional argument "input", allowing you to
        pass bytes or a string to the subprocess's stdin.  If you use this argument
        you may not also use the Popen constructor's "stdin" argument, as
        it will be used internally.
    
        By default, all communication is in bytes, and therefore any "input" should
        be bytes, and the stdout and stderr will be bytes. If in text mode, any
        "input" should be a string, and stdout and stderr will be strings decoded
        according to locale encoding, or by "encoding" if set. Text mode is
        triggered by setting any of text, encoding, errors or universal_newlines.
    
        The other arguments are the same as for the Popen constructor.
        """
        if input is not None:
            if kwargs.get('stdin') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdin and input arguments may not both be used.')
            kwargs['stdin'] = PIPE
    
        if capture_output:
            if kwargs.get('stdout') is not None or kwargs.get('stderr') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdout and stderr arguments may not be used '
                                 'with capture_output.')
            kwargs['stdout'] = PIPE
            kwargs['stderr'] = PIPE
    
        with Popen(*popenargs, **kwargs) as process:
            try:
                stdout, stderr = process.communicate(input, timeout=timeout)
            except TimeoutExpired as exc:
                process.kill()
                if _mswindows:
                    # Windows accumulates the output in a single blocking
                    # read() call run on child threads, with the timeout
                    # being done in a join() on those threads.  communicate()
                    # _after_ kill() is required to collect that and add it
                    # to the exception.
                    exc.stdout, exc.stderr = process.communicate()
                else:
                    # POSIX _communicate already populated the output so
                    # far into the TimeoutExpired exception.
                    process.wait()
                raise
            except:  # Including KeyboardInterrupt, communicate handled that.
                process.kill()
                # We don't call process.wait() as .__exit__ does that for us.
                raise
            retcode = process.poll()
            if check and retcode:
>               raise CalledProcessError(retcode, process.args,
                                         output=stdout, stderr=stderr)
E               subprocess.CalledProcessError: Command '['git', '-C', '/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-controle-ia-travail/7e8b3f41-3765-57f1-9b67-e1297e8214ec/scratchpad/routine/tmp-tests/pytest-of-root/pytest-0/course-S-018/depot', '-c', 'user.name=essai', '-c', 'user.email=essai@exemple.invalid', '-c', 'commit.gpgsign=false', 'read-tree', '5e5cb65b4daf8726ebe1c4032a836b850de7ea2f']' returned non-zero exit status 128.

        """Run command with arguments and return a CompletedProcess instance.
    
        The returned instance will have attributes args, returncode, stdout and
        stderr. By default, stdout and stderr are not captured, and those attributes
        will be None. Pass stdout=PIPE and/or stderr=PIPE in order to capture them,
        or pass capture_output=True to capture both.
    
        If check is True and the exit code was non-zero, it raises a
        CalledProcessError. The CalledProcessError object will have the return code
        in the returncode attribute, and output & stderr attributes if those streams
        were captured.
    
        If timeout (seconds) is given and the process takes too long,
         a TimeoutExpired exception will be raised.
    
        There is an optional argument "input", allowing you to
        pass bytes or a string to the subprocess's stdin.  If you use this argument
        you may not also use the Popen constructor's "stdin" argument, as
        it will be used internally.
    
        By default, all communication is in bytes, and therefore any "input" should
        be bytes, and the stdout and stderr will be bytes. If in text mode, any
        "input" should be a string, and stdout and stderr will be strings decoded
        according to locale encoding, or by "encoding" if set. Text mode is
        triggered by setting any of text, encoding, errors or universal_newlines.
    
        The other arguments are the same as for the Popen constructor.
        """
        if input is not None:
            if kwargs.get('stdin') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdin and input arguments may not both be used.')
            kwargs['stdin'] = PIPE
    
        if capture_output:
            if kwargs.get('stdout') is not None or kwargs.get('stderr') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdout and stderr arguments may not be used '
                                 'with capture_output.')
            kwargs['stdout'] = PIPE
            kwargs['stderr'] = PIPE
    
        with Popen(*popenargs, **kwargs) as process:
            try:
                stdout, stderr = process.communicate(input, timeout=timeout)
            except TimeoutExpired as exc:
                process.kill()
                if _mswindows:
                    # Windows accumulates the output in a single blocking
                    # read() call run on child threads, with the timeout
                    # being done in a join() on those threads.  communicate()
                    # _after_ kill() is required to collect that and add it
                    # to the exception.
                    exc.stdout, exc.stderr = process.communicate()
                else:
                    # POSIX _communicate already populated the output so
                    # far into the TimeoutExpired exception.
                    process.wait()
                raise
            except:  # Including KeyboardInterrupt, communicate handled that.
                process.kill()
                # We don't call process.wait() as .__exit__ does that for us.
                raise
            retcode = process.poll()
            if check and retcode:
>               raise CalledProcessError(retcode, process.args,
                                         output=stdout, stderr=stderr)

kwargs = {'env': {'TMPDIR': '/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-controle-ia-travail/7e8b3f41-3765-57f1-9b67-e1297e8214ec/scratchpad/routine/tmp-tests', 'PYTHONPATH': 'src', 'IS_SANDBOX': '1', 'SHELL': '/bin/bash', ...}, 'stdout': -1, 'stderr': -1}

    def run(*popenargs,
            input=None, capture_output=False, timeout=None, check=False, **kwargs):
        """Run command with arguments and return a CompletedProcess instance.
    
        The returned instance will have attributes args, returncode, stdout and
        stderr. By default, stdout and stderr are not captured, and those attributes
        will be None. Pass stdout=PIPE and/or stderr=PIPE in order to capture them,
        or pass capture_output=True to capture both.
    
        If check is True and the exit code was non-zero, it raises a
        CalledProcessError. The CalledProcessError object will have the return code
        in the returncode attribute, and output & stderr attributes if those streams
        were captured.
    
        If timeout (seconds) is given and the process takes too long,
         a TimeoutExpired exception will be raised.
    
        There is an optional argument "input", allowing you to
        pass bytes or a string to the subprocess's stdin.  If you use this argument
        you may not also use the Popen constructor's "stdin" argument, as
        it will be used internally.
    
        By default, all communication is in bytes, and therefore any "input" should
        be bytes, and the stdout and stderr will be bytes. If in text mode, any
        "input" should be a string, and stdout and stderr will be strings decoded
        according to locale encoding, or by "encoding" if set. Text mode is
        triggered by setting any of text, encoding, errors or universal_newlines.
    
        The other arguments are the same as for the Popen constructor.
        """
        if input is not None:
            if kwargs.get('stdin') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdin and input arguments may not both be used.')
            kwargs['stdin'] = PIPE
    
        if capture_output:
            if kwargs.get('stdout') is not None or kwargs.get('stderr') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdout and stderr arguments may not be used '
                                 'with capture_output.')
            kwargs['stdout'] = PIPE
            kwargs['stderr'] = PIPE
    
        with Popen(*popenargs, **kwargs) as process:
            try:
                stdout, stderr = process.communicate(input, timeout=timeout)
            except TimeoutExpired as exc:
                process.kill()
                if _mswindows:
                    # Windows accumulates the output in a single blocking
                    # read() call run on child threads, with the timeout
                    # being done in a join() on those threads.  communicate()
                    # _after_ kill() is required to collect that and add it
                    # to the exception.
                    exc.stdout, exc.stderr = process.communicate()
                else:
                    # POSIX _communicate already populated the output so
                    # far into the TimeoutExpired exception.
                    process.wait()
                raise
            except:  # Including KeyboardInterrupt, communicate handled that.
                process.kill()
                # We don't call process.wait() as .__exit__ does that for us.
                raise
            retcode = process.poll()
            if check and retcode:
>               raise CalledProcessError(retcode, process.args,
                                         output=stdout, stderr=stderr)
E               subprocess.CalledProcessError: Command '['git', '-C', '/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-controle-ia-travail/7e8b3f41-3765-57f1-9b67-e1297e8214ec/scratchpad/routine/tmp-tests/pytest-of-root/pytest-0/course-R-05/depot', '-c', 'user.name=essai', '-c', 'user.email=essai@exemple.invalid', '-c', 'commit.gpgsign=false', 'read-tree', '5e5cb65b4daf8726ebe1c4032a836b850de7ea2f']' returned non-zero exit status 128.

    def run(*popenargs,
            input=None, capture_output=False, timeout=None, check=False, **kwargs):
        """Run command with arguments and return a CompletedProcess instance.
    
        The returned instance will have attributes args, returncode, stdout and
        stderr. By default, stdout and stderr are not captured, and those attributes
        will be None. Pass stdout=PIPE and/or stderr=PIPE in order to capture them,
        or pass capture_output=True to capture both.
    
        If check is True and the exit code was non-zero, it raises a
        CalledProcessError. The CalledProcessError object will have the return code
        in the returncode attribute, and output & stderr attributes if those streams
        were captured.
    
        If timeout (seconds) is given and the process takes too long,
         a TimeoutExpired exception will be raised.
    
        There is an optional argument "input", allowing you to
        pass bytes or a string to the subprocess's stdin.  If you use this argument
        you may not also use the Popen constructor's "stdin" argument, as
        it will be used internally.
    
        By default, all communication is in bytes, and therefore any "input" should
        be bytes, and the stdout and stderr will be bytes. If in text mode, any
        "input" should be a string, and stdout and stderr will be strings decoded
        according to locale encoding, or by "encoding" if set. Text mode is
        triggered by setting any of text, encoding, errors or universal_newlines.
    
        The other arguments are the same as for the Popen constructor.
        """
        if input is not None:
            if kwargs.get('stdin') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdin and input arguments may not both be used.')
            kwargs['stdin'] = PIPE
    
        if capture_output:
            if kwargs.get('stdout') is not None or kwargs.get('stderr') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdout and stderr arguments may not be used '
                                 'with capture_output.')
            kwargs['stdout'] = PIPE
            kwargs['stderr'] = PIPE
    
        with Popen(*popenargs, **kwargs) as process:
            try:
                stdout, stderr = process.communicate(input, timeout=timeout)
            except TimeoutExpired as exc:
                process.kill()
                if _mswindows:
                    # Windows accumulates the output in a single blocking
                    # read() call run on child threads, with the timeout
                    # being done in a join() on those threads.  communicate()
                    # _after_ kill() is required to collect that and add it
                    # to the exception.
                    exc.stdout, exc.stderr = process.communicate()
                else:
                    # POSIX _communicate already populated the output so
                    # far into the TimeoutExpired exception.
                    process.wait()
                raise
            except:  # Including KeyboardInterrupt, communicate handled that.
                process.kill()
                # We don't call process.wait() as .__exit__ does that for us.
                raise
            retcode = process.poll()
            if check and retcode:
>               raise CalledProcessError(retcode, process.args,
                                         output=stdout, stderr=stderr)

        be bytes, and the stdout and stderr will be bytes. If in text mode, any

        according to locale encoding, or by "encoding" if set. Text mode is
        triggered by setting any of text, encoding, errors or universal_newlines.
    
        The other arguments are the same as for the Popen constructor.

            kwargs['stdin'] = PIPE

            kwargs['stdout'] = PIPE
            kwargs['stderr'] = PIPE

            except TimeoutExpired as exc:

                    # being done in a join() on those threads.  communicate()

        according to locale encoding, or by "encoding" if set. Text mode is
        triggered by setting any of text, encoding, errors or universal_newlines.

            kwargs['stdin'] = PIPE

            kwargs['stderr'] = PIPE

    def run(*popenargs,

        """Run command with arguments and return a CompletedProcess instance.
    
        The returned instance will have attributes args, returncode, stdout and
        stderr. By default, stdout and stderr are not captured, and those attributes
        will be None. Pass stdout=PIPE and/or stderr=PIPE in order to capture them,
        or pass capture_output=True to capture both.

        CalledProcessError. The CalledProcessError object will have the return code
        in the returncode attribute, and output & stderr attributes if those streams
        were captured.

         a TimeoutExpired exception will be raised.

        pass bytes or a string to the subprocess's stdin.  If you use this argument
        you may not also use the Popen constructor's "stdin" argument, as
        it will be used internally.

    def run(*popenargs,
            input=None, capture_output=False, timeout=None, check=False, **kwargs):
        """Run command with arguments and return a CompletedProcess instance.
    
        The returned instance will have attributes args, returncode, stdout and
        stderr. By default, stdout and stderr are not captured, and those attributes
        will be None. Pass stdout=PIPE and/or stderr=PIPE in order to capture them,
        or pass capture_output=True to capture both.
    
        If check is True and the exit code was non-zero, it raises a
        CalledProcessError. The CalledProcessError object will have the return code
        in the returncode attribute, and output & stderr attributes if those streams
        were captured.
    
        If timeout (seconds) is given and the process takes too long,
         a TimeoutExpired exception will be raised.
    
        There is an optional argument "input", allowing you to
        pass bytes or a string to the subprocess's stdin.  If you use this argument
        you may not also use the Popen constructor's "stdin" argument, as
        it will be used internally.
    
        By default, all communication is in bytes, and therefore any "input" should
        be bytes, and the stdout and stderr will be bytes. If in text mode, any
        "input" should be a string, and stdout and stderr will be strings decoded
        according to locale encoding, or by "encoding" if set. Text mode is
        triggered by setting any of text, encoding, errors or universal_newlines.
    
        The other arguments are the same as for the Popen constructor.
        """
        if input is not None:
            if kwargs.get('stdin') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdin and input arguments may not both be used.')
            kwargs['stdin'] = PIPE
    
        if capture_output:
            if kwargs.get('stdout') is not None or kwargs.get('stderr') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdout and stderr arguments may not be used '
                                 'with capture_output.')
            kwargs['stdout'] = PIPE
            kwargs['stderr'] = PIPE
    
        with Popen(*popenargs, **kwargs) as process:
            try:
                stdout, stderr = process.communicate(input, timeout=timeout)
            except TimeoutExpired as exc:
                process.kill()
                if _mswindows:
                    # Windows accumulates the output in a single blocking
                    # read() call run on child threads, with the timeout
                    # being done in a join() on those threads.  communicate()
                    # _after_ kill() is required to collect that and add it
                    # to the exception.
                    exc.stdout, exc.stderr = process.communicate()
                else:
                    # POSIX _communicate already populated the output so
                    # far into the TimeoutExpired exception.
                    process.wait()
                raise
            except:  # Including KeyboardInterrupt, communicate handled that.
                process.kill()
                # We don't call process.wait() as .__exit__ does that for us.
                raise
            retcode = process.poll()
            if check and retcode:
>               raise CalledProcessError(retcode, process.args,
                                         output=stdout, stderr=stderr)

    def run(*popenargs,
            input=None, capture_output=False, timeout=None, check=False, **kwargs):
        """Run command with arguments and return a CompletedProcess instance.
    
        The returned instance will have attributes args, returncode, stdout and
        stderr. By default, stdout and stderr are not captured, and those attributes
        will be None. Pass stdout=PIPE and/or stderr=PIPE in order to capture them,
        or pass capture_output=True to capture both.
    
        If check is True and the exit code was non-zero, it raises a
        CalledProcessError. The CalledProcessError object will have the return code
        in the returncode attribute, and output & stderr attributes if those streams
        were captured.
    
        If timeout (seconds) is given and the process takes too long,
         a TimeoutExpired exception will be raised.
    
        There is an optional argument "input", allowing you to
        pass bytes or a string to the subprocess's stdin.  If you use this argument
        you may not also use the Popen constructor's "stdin" argument, as
        it will be used internally.
    
        By default, all communication is in bytes, and therefore any "input" should
        be bytes, and the stdout and stderr will be bytes. If in text mode, any
        "input" should be a string, and stdout and stderr will be strings decoded
        according to locale encoding, or by "encoding" if set. Text mode is
        triggered by setting any of text, encoding, errors or universal_newlines.
    
        The other arguments are the same as for the Popen constructor.
        """
        if input is not None:
            if kwargs.get('stdin') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdin and input arguments may not both be used.')
            kwargs['stdin'] = PIPE
    
        if capture_output:
            if kwargs.get('stdout') is not None or kwargs.get('stderr') is not None:
                raise ValueError('stdout and stderr arguments may not be used '
                                 'with capture_output.')
            kwargs['stdout'] = PIPE
            kwargs['stderr'] = PIPE
    
        with Popen(*popenargs, **kwargs) as process:
            try:
                stdout, stderr = process.communicate(input, timeout=timeout)
            except TimeoutExpired as exc:
                process.kill()
                if _mswindows:
                    # Windows accumulates the output in a single blocking
                    # read() call run on child threads, with the timeout
                    # being done in a join() on those threads.  communicate()
                    # _after_ kill() is required to collect that and add it
                    # to the exception.
                    exc.stdout, exc.stderr = process.communicate()
                else:
                    # POSIX _communicate already populated the output so
                    # far into the TimeoutExpired exception.
                    process.wait()
                raise
            except:  # Including KeyboardInterrupt, communicate handled that.
                process.kill()
                # We don't call process.wait() as .__exit__ does that for us.
                raise
            retcode = process.poll()
            if check and retcode:
>               raise CalledProcessError(retcode, process.args,
                                         output=stdout, stderr=stderr)
E               subprocess.CalledProcessError: Command '['git', '-C', '/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-controle-ia-travail/7e8b3f41-3765-57f1-9b67-e1297e8214ec/scratchpad/routine/tmp-tests/pytest-of-root/pytest-0/course-S-028/depot', '-c', 'user.name=essai', '-c', 'user.email=essai@exemple.invalid', '-c', 'commit.gpgsign=false', 'read-tree', '5e5cb65b4daf8726ebe1c4032a836b850de7ea2f']' returned non-zero exit status 128.

    def lever_sur_term(signum, frame) -> None:
        """Gestionnaire du signal TERM des lanceurs de T0.5 : pose le drapeau, puis lève `GardeArret`. Une bibliothèque peut
        avaler l'exception levée pendant son code, ou la convertir (import paresseux de torch, pendant la mise en place :
        vu le 2026-10-10, relecture de la revalidation, D-1). Le drapeau reste alors posé : `exiger_sans_signal` le relève
        aux points de contrôle (chaque pas des épisodes, chaque lot des notations et de l'appariement, avant le repère,
        avant le résumé), et le bloc qui consigne l'arrêt écrit le motif « signal TERM » quelle que soit l'exception.
        Seul le premier signal lève : `timeout` relaie TERM au processus puis à son groupe, et un second signal ne doit pas
        interrompre la consignation de l'arrêt (vérification 2 du remplissage, K-1) ; il pose le drapeau, relevé comme le
        premier aux points de contrôle."""
        premier = not SIGNAL_TERM_RECU
        SIGNAL_TERM_RECU.append(signum)
        if premier:
